```
36	
37	/// Named stages of the `push` state machine.
38	///
39	/// Used by `--from <stage>` to resume at a specific point and by
40	/// `--status` to report the current position. Ordered top-down so
41	/// `Stage as u8` comparisons reflect progress through the flow.
42	#[derive(Copy, Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, ValueEnum)]
43	#[value(rename_all = "kebab-case")]
44	pub enum Stage {
45	    /// Run fmt / clippy / test / install / retest.
46	    Preflight,
47	    /// Present diff for the first approval gate.
48	    Review,
49	    /// Compose / edit the commit message; present for second gate.
50	    Message,
51	    /// Commit the app repo.
52	    CommitApp,
53	    /// Commit the `.claude` session repo (skipped if empty).
54	    CommitClaude,
55	    /// Advance both bookmarks to `@-`.
56	    BookmarkBoth,
57	    /// `jj git push --bookmark <b> -R .`.
58	    PushApp,
59	    /// `vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <b> ...`.
60	    FinalizeClaude,
61	}
62	
63	impl Stage {
64	    /// Return the stage's kebab-case string identifier (matches what
65	    /// the CLI accepts via `--from <stage>` and what `PushState`
66	    /// persists to disk).
67	    pub fn as_str(self) -> &'static str {
68	        match self {
69	            Stage::Preflight => "preflight",
70	            Stage::Review => "review",
71	            Stage::Message => "message",
72	            Stage::CommitApp => "commit-app",
73	            Stage::CommitClaude => "commit-claude",
74	            Stage::BookmarkBoth => "bookmark-both",
75	            Stage::PushApp => "push-app",
76	            Stage::FinalizeClaude => "finalize-claude",
77	        }
78	    }
79	
80	    /// Parse a kebab-case stage name back into a `Stage`.
81	    ///
82	    /// Unknown names return `None`; callers should surface a helpful
83	    /// error rather than silently substituting a default.
84	    pub fn from_str(name: &str) -> Option<Self> {
85	        match name {
86	            "preflight" => Some(Stage::Preflight),
87	            "review" => Some(Stage::Review),
88	            "message" => Some(Stage::Message),
89	            "commit-app" => Some(Stage::CommitApp),
90	            "commit-claude" => Some(Stage::CommitClaude),
91	            "bookmark-both" => Some(Stage::BookmarkBoth),
92	            "push-app" => Some(Stage::PushApp),
93	            "finalize-claude" => Some(Stage::FinalizeClaude),
94	            _ => None,
95	        }
96	    }
97	
98	    /// The stage that follows this one, or `None` when this is the
99	    /// last stage (`FinalizeClaude`).
100	    pub fn next(self) -> Option<Self> {
101	        match self {
102	            Stage::Preflight => Some(Stage::Review),
103	            Stage::Review => Some(Stage::Message),
104	            Stage::Message => Some(Stage::CommitApp),
105	            Stage::CommitApp => Some(Stage::CommitClaude),
106	            Stage::CommitClaude => Some(Stage::BookmarkBoth),
107	            Stage::BookmarkBoth => Some(Stage::PushApp),
108	            Stage::PushApp => Some(Stage::FinalizeClaude),
109	            Stage::FinalizeClaude => None,
110	        }
111	    }
112	
113	    /// The first stage in the flow — used when no saved state exists.
114	    pub fn first() -> Self {
115	        Stage::Preflight
116	    }
117	}
118	
119	/// CLI arguments for the `push` subcommand.
120	///
121	/// Flag set mirrors the design in `notes/chores-05.md`. Flags are
122	/// parsed in 0.37.0-0; their real effects land across the remaining
123	/// 0.37.0-N steps. See the module docstring for which flags activate
124	/// when.
```

---

## Plan: 0.42.0-4 — `push --scope`

**Goal.** Make push's state machine scope-aware on top of the new `Scope` enum. Add `--scope` / `-s` to `PushArgs`, persist scope in `PushState` so resumes can't silently switch shape, and have each stage run or no-op based on scope.

**Scope semantics for push.**

| Scope | Runs |
|---|---|
| `Roles([Code, Bot])` (today's default in dual workspaces) | all 8 stages, ochid trailers on both commits |
| `Roles([Code])` | preflight (code only), review (code), message (code chid only), commit-app (no ochid), bookmark code, push code, **skip** commit-claude / finalize-claude |
| `Roles([Bot])` | preflight (bot only), review (bot), message (bot chid only), commit-bot, bookmark bot, push bot, **skip** commit-app / push-app / finalize |
| `Single(<path>)` | preflight (path), review (path), message (path chid), commit-path, bookmark path, push path, **skip** finalize |

Single-side and `Single` modes drop the `ochid:` trailer (no counterpart commit to point at).

**Edits.**

- `Cargo.toml` — `0.42.0-3` → `0.42.0-4`.
- `src/push.rs`:
  - `PushArgs`: add `scope: Option<Scope>` (short `-s`, `value_parser = parse_scope`, `verbatim_doc_comment`).
  - `PushState`: add `scope: Option<Scope>` (serialized to TOML — keyword form for `Roles`, path string for `Single`); on resume, if `--scope` differs, error "scope mismatch — `--restart`".
  - Bump `STATE_FORMAT_VERSION` to `2`; v1 state files refuse to load with the same "`--restart`" remediation.
  - Introduce `resolve_repos(workspace_root, scope) -> Vec<PathBuf>` (small wrapper over `scope_to_repos`) and thread a resolved `(code_path?, bot_path?)` through the stages.
  - Each stage gets a scope guard. The stage names stay as-is (`commit-claude`, `bookmark-both`, `finalize-claude`) — not renaming this cycle keeps the diff small and the state-format rationale singular (resume safety, not naming churn).
  - `verify_state_sanity` / `verify_completion_sanity`: only check the chids/WC that the active scope produced.
- Integration tests in `src/push.rs`: cover `Roles([Code])` and one `Single(_)` shape end-to-end against the existing `Fixture`. (`Roles([Bot])` if it falls out cheaply — can defer to dogfood otherwise.)
- `notes/todo.md` — flip `-3` to `(done)`, `-4` to `(current)`.
- `notes/chores-07.md` — add `### 0.42.0-4: push --scope` post-impl subsection.

**Open design questions before I start.**

1. **Bot-only finalize.** `finalize-claude` exists to detach a squash for *trailing* session writes after foreground push exits. On `Roles([Bot])` push, push *is* the bot operation — no trailing writes from another command. **Recommend:** skip `finalize-claude` on bot-only too. Agree, or want the detached squash to still run "for symmetry"?

2. **Stage renames.** `commit-claude` / `bookmark-both` / `finalize-claude` read awkwardly under `code|bot` vocab and `Single` mode. **Recommend:** leave them this cycle — renaming would compound the state-format bump and inflate the diff. A naming sweep can be its own future step. OK to defer?

3. **State format bump (v1 → v2).** Recommend loud refusal of v1 state with `--restart` remediation rather than back-fill `scope = "code,bot"`. Worst-case impact: anyone with a parked push-state.toml gets one prompt to restart. Acceptable?

Awaiting your read on the three questions (or any other shape changes you'd want) before I touch code.

> DEVELOPER

1. The bot should not use "push", it needs to follow the finalize protocol. But others may as they will not be modifying the bots repo, .claude at the same time.
2,3. this change is about "push" which is separate from "finalize", correct

> AGENT

Got it. Confirming my reading:

- **Q1**: When scope includes the bot side AND the bot agent is the one running push (today's default `Roles([Code, Bot])` case), `finalize-claude` is mandatory because session writes are concurrent. For `Roles([Bot])` invoked by a human/script — no concurrent writes → direct foreground commit/push is fine, no finalize. So the matrix matches my original recommendation: finalize runs only on `Roles([Code, Bot])`.
- **Q2/Q3**: Yes — push is separate from finalize. Stage renames stay deferred; state format bump (v1→v2 with loud refusal) goes ahead.

Plan locked. Starting now.

```
367	    /// Load state from `path`, returning `Ok(None)` if the file is
368	    /// absent (a fresh run) and `Err` if the file exists but is
369	    /// unusable (stale format, missing required keys, unknown stage).
370	    pub fn load(path: &Path) -> Result<Option<Self>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
371	        if !path.exists() {
372	            return Ok(None);
373	        }
374	        let map = toml_load(path)?;
375	        let require = |k: &str| -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
376	            map.get(k)
377	                .cloned()
378	                .ok_or_else(|| format!("push state {}: missing key '{k}'", path.display()).into())
379	        };
380	        let version: u32 = require("push-state.version")?
381	            .parse()
382	            .map_err(|e| format!("push state {}: invalid version: {e}", path.display()))?;
383	        if version != STATE_FORMAT_VERSION {
384	            return Err(format!(
385	                "push state {}: unsupported format version {version} (expected {}); \
386	                 re-run with --restart to start fresh",
387	                path.display(),
388	                STATE_FORMAT_VERSION
389	            )
390	            .into());
391	        }
392	        let stage_str = require("push-state.stage")?;
393	        let stage = Stage::from_str(&stage_str)
394	            .ok_or_else(|| format!("push state {}: unknown stage '{stage_str}'", path.display()))?;
395	        let claude_had_changes = match map.get("push-state.claude_had_changes") {
396	            Some(s) => Some(s.parse::<bool>().map_err(|e| {
397	                format!(
398	                    "push state {}: invalid claude_had_changes: {e}",
399	                    path.display()
400	                )
401	            })?),
402	            None => None,
403	        };
404	        Ok(Some(PushState {
405	            version,
406	            stage,
407	            bookmark: require("push-state.bookmark")?,
408	            started_at: require("push-state.started_at")?,

414	            title: map.get("push-state.title").map(|s| unescape_multiline(s)),
415	            body: map.get("push-state.body").map(|s| unescape_multiline(s)),
416	        }))
417	    }
418	}
419	
420	/// Escape any `"` in a value so the single-line TOML strings we emit
421	/// stay parseable. `toml_simple` trims the surrounding quotes on read
422	/// but doesn't process escapes, so we just avoid characters that
423	/// would break the single-line form.
424	fn escape_toml(s: &str) -> String {
425	    s.replace('"', "\\\"")
426	}
427	
428	/// Escape a potentially multi-line string for persistence in a
429	/// single-line TOML value. `toml_simple` only handles one line per
430	/// value so we encode newlines as `\n`, tabs as `\t`, and any stray
431	/// `"` / `\` as their escaped forms. The inverse is
432	/// `unescape_multiline`.
433	fn escape_multiline(s: &str) -> String {
434	    let mut out = String::with_capacity(s.len());
435	    for ch in s.chars() {
436	        match ch {
437	            '\\' => out.push_str("\\\\"),
438	            '"' => out.push_str("\\\""),
439	            '\n' => out.push_str("\\n"),
440	            '\t' => out.push_str("\\t"),
441	            '\r' => out.push_str("\\r"),
442	            c => out.push(c),
443	        }
444	    }
445	    out
446	}
447	
448	/// Invert `escape_multiline`. Unknown backslash-escapes pass through
449	/// untouched (best-effort — this is a managed state file, so it's
450	/// unlikely to encounter hand-edited escapes).
451	fn unescape_multiline(s: &str) -> String {
452	    let mut out = String::with_capacity(s.len());
453	    let mut chars = s.chars();
454	    while let Some(ch) = chars.next() {
455	        if ch == '\\' {
456	            match chars.next() {
457	                Some('\\') => out.push('\\'),
458	                Some('"') => out.push('"'),
459	                Some('n') => out.push('\n'),
460	                Some('t') => out.push('\t'),
461	                Some('r') => out.push('\r'),
462	                Some(other) => {
463	                    out.push('\\');
464	                    out.push(other);
465	                }
466	                None => out.push('\\'),
467	            }
468	        } else {
469	            out.push(ch);
470	        }
471	    }
472	    out
473	}
474	
475	/// Whether `stdin` is attached to a terminal.
476	///
477	/// Interactive prompts (`stage_review`, `$EDITOR` launch in
478	/// `stage_message`, `--step` gates) need this to fail fast in
479	/// scripted / CI contexts instead of hanging on `read_line`.
480	/// `--yes` overrides the check — script callers opt in by
481	/// asserting "all prompts auto-approved".
482	fn is_stdin_tty() -> bool {
483	    std::io::stdin().is_terminal()
484	}
485	
486	/// Verify the configured state dir is matched by a `.gitignore` entry,
487	/// erroring if not. The check is intentionally simple — looks for
488	/// `<state_dir>` or `/<state_dir>` as a line in `.gitignore` at the
489	/// repo root. Misses trickier patterns (nested wildcards, ignore files
490	/// further down the tree) but catches the common "user changed the
491	/// config and forgot to update .gitignore" case, which is the whole
492	/// point. Fatal as of 0.37.1 — the warning was easy to miss in the
493	/// preflight wall of output, and a committed state file is a real
494	/// foot-gun. `vc-x1 init` and `vc-x1 test-fixture` write `/.vc-x1`
495	/// into their generated `.gitignore` so the fresh-repo path is clean.
496	fn check_gitignore_coherence(
497	    root: &Path,
498	    state_dir_name: &str,
499	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
500	    let gitignore = root.join(".gitignore");
501	    let matched = match fs::read_to_string(&gitignore) {
502	        Ok(content) => content.lines().any(|line| {
503	            let l = line.trim();
504	            l == state_dir_name
505	                || l == format!("/{state_dir_name}")
506	                || l == format!("{state_dir_name}/")
507	                || l == format!("/{state_dir_name}/")
508	        }),
509	        Err(_) => false,
510	    };
511	    if !matched {
512	        return Err(format!(
513	            "push: state dir '{state_dir_name}' is not in {} — \
514	             add a '/{state_dir_name}' line so in-progress push-state \
515	             files don't get committed",
516	            gitignore.display()
517	        )
518	        .into());
519	    }
520	    Ok(())
521	}
522	
523	/// Entry point for the `push` subcommand.
524	///
```

```
1848	    /// `escape_multiline` round-trips via `unescape_multiline`
1849	    /// across every escape case we emit.
1850	    #[test]
1851	    fn multiline_escape_roundtrip() {
1852	        for original in [
1853	            "",
1854	            "simple",
1855	            "line one\nline two",
1856	            "tab\tseparated\tvalues",
1857	            "quoted \"text\" inside",
1858	            "backslash \\ here",
1859	            "mixed:\n\t\"quoted\"\\ path\r\n",
1860	        ] {
1861	            let escaped = escape_multiline(original);
1862	            // Escaped form contains no raw newlines, tabs, or CRs —
1863	            // safe for our single-line TOML value slot.
1864	            assert!(!escaped.contains('\n'), "escape leaked \\n: {escaped:?}");
1865	            assert!(!escaped.contains('\t'), "escape leaked \\t: {escaped:?}");
1866	            assert!(!escaped.contains('\r'), "escape leaked \\r: {escaped:?}");
1867	            assert_eq!(unescape_multiline(&escaped), original);
1868	        }
1869	    }

1871	    /// `parse_message` extracts title + body, strips `#` comments,
1872	    /// and rejects all-comments / empty input.
1873	    #[test]
1874	    fn parse_message_cases() {
1875	        // Title + body separated by blank line.
1876	        let (t, b) = parse_message("feat: x\n\nBody here.\nSecond line.\n").unwrap();
1877	        assert_eq!(t, "feat: x");
1878	        assert_eq!(b, "Body here.\nSecond line.");
1879	
1880	        // Title + body with no blank line — first line is title, rest is body.
1881	        let (t, b) = parse_message("feat: x\nBody here.\nSecond line.\n").unwrap();
1882	        assert_eq!(t, "feat: x");
1883	        assert_eq!(b, "Body here.\nSecond line.");
1884	
1885	        // Body with internal blank lines preserved.
1886	        let (t, b) = parse_message("feat: x\npara 1\n\npara 2\n").unwrap();
1887	        assert_eq!(t, "feat: x");
1888	        assert_eq!(b, "para 1\n\npara 2");
1889	
1890	        // Comments stripped.
1891	        let (t, b) =
1892	            parse_message("# comment\nfeat: y\n# mid-comment\n\nbody\n# tail-comment\n").unwrap();
1893	        assert_eq!(t, "feat: y");
1894	        assert_eq!(b, "body");
1895	
1896	        // Title only (no body).
1897	        let (t, b) = parse_message("feat: z\n").unwrap();
1898	        assert_eq!(t, "feat: z");
1899	        assert_eq!(b, "");
1900	
1901	        // All comments → None (caller aborts).
1902	        assert!(parse_message("# only comments\n# and more\n").is_none());
1903	        // All blank → None.
1904	        assert!(parse_message("   \n\n").is_none());
1905	    }
1906	}

1911	    //! fixtures (bare-git remotes + colocated jj repos under a

1913	    //!
1914	    //! Every test uses `--from message` to skip `preflight` (no
1915	    //! `Cargo.toml` in the fixture) and `--no-finalize` to avoid
1916	    //! spawning a detached `vc-x1 finalize` child that would
1917	    //! outlive the test. The remaining stages (message,
1918	    //! commit-app, commit-claude, bookmark-both, push-app) are
1919	    //! exercised against the fixture's local bare-git remote.

1921	    //! Stage execution + rollback are covered here;

1932	    /// Run `jj <args> -R <repo>` and return trimmed stdout on success.
1933	    fn jj(repo: &Path, args: &[&str]) -> String {
1934	        let out = Command::new("jj")
1935	            .args(args)
1936	            .arg("-R")
1937	            .arg(repo)
1938	            .output()
1939	            .expect("spawn jj");
1940	        assert!(
1941	            out.status.success(),
1942	            "jj {args:?} failed in {}: {}",
1943	            repo.display(),
1944	            String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr)
1945	        );
1946	        String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout).trim().to_string()
1947	    }

1954	        )

2067	    /// `rollback_on_failure` rewinds both repos to their recorded
2068	    /// `jj op` snapshots when triggered mid-flow.
2069	    ///
2070	    /// Simulates a failure after both repos have had their `main`
2071	    /// bookmark advanced past the original position, then calls
2072	    /// `rollback_on_failure` with the pre-mutation op IDs. After
2073	    /// rollback, `main` should be back at the starting commit in
2074	    /// both repos.
2075	    ///
2076	    /// Notes:
2077	    /// - We don't compare `current_op_id` post-rollback because
2078	    ///   reading the op id snapshots the (still-dirty) working
2079	    ///   copy, creating a fresh op. Bookmark position is the
2080	    ///   load-bearing invariant anyway.
2081	    /// - Each mutation sequence actually moves `main` (describe →
2082	    ///   bookmark set → new) so the pre-rollback state is
2083	    ///   observably different from the post-rollback state.
2084	    #[test]
2085	    fn push_rollback_restores_both_repos() {
2086	        let fx = Fixture::new("push-rollback");
2087	
2088	        // Snapshot pre-mutation state.
2089	        let op_app_start = current_op_id(&fx.work).expect("app op id");
2090	        let op_claude_start = current_op_id(&fx.claude).expect("claude op id");
2091	        let main_app_start = cid(&fx.work, "main");
2092	        let main_claude_start = cid(&fx.claude, "main");
2093	
2094	        // Mutate both repos so `main` actually advances (this is
2095	        // what rollback has to undo).
2096	        fs::write(fx.work.join("app.txt"), "app").expect("write");
2097	        fs::write(fx.claude.join("session.jsonl"), "{}\n").expect("write");
2098	        jj(&fx.work, &["describe", "-m", "test commit"]);
2099	        jj(&fx.work, &["bookmark", "set", "main", "-r", "@"]);
2100	        jj(&fx.work, &["new"]);
2101	        jj(&fx.claude, &["describe", "-m", "test session"]);
2102	        jj(&fx.claude, &["bookmark", "set", "main", "-r", "@"]);
2103	        jj(&fx.claude, &["new"]);
2104	
2105	        // Sanity: main has advanced in both repos.
2106	        assert_ne!(
2107	            cid(&fx.work, "main"),
2108	            main_app_start,
2109	            "setup should have moved app main"
2110	        );
2111	        assert_ne!(
2112	            cid(&fx.claude, "main"),
2113	            main_claude_start,
2114	            "setup should have moved .claude main"
2115	        );
2116	
2117	        // State records we're at bookmark-both with snapshots from
2118	        // before any of the above mutations.
2119	        let state = PushState {
2120	            version: STATE_FORMAT_VERSION,
2121	            stage: Stage::BookmarkBoth,
2122	            bookmark: "main".to_string(),
2123	            started_at: "2026-04-21T20:00:00+00:00".to_string(),
2124	            app_chid: None,
2125	            claude_chid: None,
2126	            claude_had_changes: Some(true),
2127	            op_app: Some(op_app_start),
2128	            op_claude: Some(op_claude_start),
2129	            title: None,
2130	            body: None,
2131	        };
2132	
2133	        let err: Box<dyn std::error::Error> = "forced for test".into();
2134	        rollback_on_failure(&fx.work, &state, err.as_ref());
2135	
2136	        // After rollback, `main` is restored in both repos.
2137	        assert_eq!(cid(&fx.work, "main"), main_app_start);
2138	        assert_eq!(cid(&fx.claude, "main"), main_claude_start);
2139	    }
```

```
1	//! Shared test helpers for dual-repo integration tests.
2	//!
3	//! Provides a `Fixture` that wraps `crate::init::init_with_symlink`
4	//! plus a per-process unique-tempdir helper so parallel tests don't
5	//! collide. Lifted out of the `sync` test module (originally inline at
6	//! `sync.rs:521–560`) so `push`'s tests (0.37.0) and any future
7	//! subcommand's tests can sit on the same harness without
8	//! copy-paste drift.
9	//!
10	//! Test-only — the whole module is gated at its declaration site via
11	//! `#[cfg(test)] mod test_helpers;`.
12	
13	use std::fs;
14	use std::path::PathBuf;
15	use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicU64, Ordering};
16	use std::time::{SystemTime, UNIX_EPOCH};
17	
18	use crate::common::write_file;
19	use crate::init::{InitArgs, init_with_symlink};
20	
21	/// Per-process counter so same-nanosecond tempdir collisions yield
22	/// distinct paths when tests run in parallel.
23	static COUNTER: AtomicU64 = AtomicU64::new(0);
24	
25	/// Build a unique tempdir path for a test fixture.
26	///
27	/// Combines a nanosecond timestamp with a per-process atomic counter
28	/// so parallel tests and same-nanosecond collisions both yield
29	/// distinct paths. Prefix is `vc-x1-test-<tag>-<ts>-<n>` so callers
30	/// from different subcommand tests stay discoverable in `$TMPDIR`.
31	pub fn unique_base(tag: &str) -> PathBuf {
32	    let ts = SystemTime::now()
33	        .duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)
34	        .map(|d| d.as_nanos())
35	        .unwrap_or(0); // OK: clock error → 0 is harmless for unique tempdir naming
36	    let n = COUNTER.fetch_add(1, Ordering::SeqCst);
37	    std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("vc-x1-test-{tag}-{ts}-{n}"))
38	}
39	
40	/// Owned dual-repo fixture with RAII cleanup.
41	///
42	/// Builds a fresh throwaway workspace under a unique tempdir by
43	/// driving `init::init_with_symlink` in `--repo-local` mode with
44	/// `create_symlink=false`, exposing the two repo paths (`work` and
45	/// `work/.claude`). The tempdir tree is removed when the value is
46	/// dropped, so a panicking test still cleans up after itself.
47	pub struct Fixture {
48	    /// Root tempdir that owns both repos and their bare-git remotes.
49	    pub base: PathBuf,
50	    /// Code repo path (`<base>/work`).
51	    pub work: PathBuf,
52	    /// Bot session repo path (`<base>/work/.claude`).
53	    pub claude: PathBuf,
54	}
55	
56	impl Fixture {
57	    /// Build a fresh fixture in a unique tempdir.
58	    pub fn new(tag: &str) -> Self {
59	        Self::new_opts(tag, false, None)
60	    }
61	
62	    /// Build a fresh fixture with optional pending changes and a
63	    /// template seed.
64	    ///
65	    /// - `with_pending` — after init, write a small file into each
66	    ///   repo so `@` carries uncommitted changes (useful for
67	    ///   finalize / push tests).
68	    /// - `use_template` — `CODE[,BOT]` forwarded to init.
69	    pub fn new_opts(tag: &str, with_pending: bool, use_template: Option<String>) -> Self {
70	        let base = unique_base(tag);
71	        // init refuses to reuse an existing project_dir, so `base`
72	        // must not exist yet. `unique_base` already guarantees that.
73	        let args = InitArgs {
74	            name: Some("work".to_string()),
75	            owner: None,
76	            dir: None,
77	            private: false,
78	            dry_run: false,
79	            push_retries: 5,
80	            push_retry_delay: 3,
81	            use_template,
82	            repo_local: Some(base.to_string_lossy().into_owned()),
83	            repo_remote: None,
84	            scope: None, // default → code,bot
85	        };
86	        init_with_symlink(&args, false).expect("build test fixture via init");
87	
88	        let work = base.join("work");
89	        let claude = work.join(".claude");
90	
91	        if with_pending {
92	            write_file(&work.join("TODO.md"), "# TODO\n- first feature\n")
93	                .expect("write pending TODO.md");
94	            write_file(
95	                &claude.join("session-notes.md"),
96	                "# Session notes\n- simulated pending work\n",
97	            )
98	            .expect("write pending session-notes.md");
99	        }
100	
101	        Fixture { base, work, claude }
102	    }
103	
104	    /// Convenience: return both repo paths as a `Vec<PathBuf>`
105	    /// suitable for `sync_repos` (or any other `&[PathBuf]` API).
106	    pub fn repos(&self) -> Vec<PathBuf> {
107	        vec![self.work.clone(), self.claude.clone()]
108	    }
109	}
110	
111	impl Drop for Fixture {
112	    /// Remove the fixture tree on drop. Best-effort; a failure here
113	    /// doesn't fail the test.
114	    fn drop(&mut self) {
115	        let _ = fs::remove_dir_all(&self.base);
116	    }
117	}
118	
```

```json
  "new_string": "    /// Roles arm including both sides — full dual-repo op.\n    pub fn is_both(&self) -> bool {\n        self.has_code() && self.has_bot()\n    }\n}\n\n/// Render a `Scope` as the canonical string form `parse_scope` accepts.\n///\n/// - `Roles(_)` → `code` / `bot` / `code,bot` / `bot,code` (preserves\n///   original side ordering).\n/// - `Single(p)` → the path's lossy display form. The stored path\n///   already carries a parser-recognized prefix (the parser is the\n///   only constructor of `Single`), so the round-trip through\n///   `parse_scope` is well-defined.\nimpl fmt::Display for Scope {\n    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n        match self {\n            Scope::Roles(sides) => {\n                let parts: Vec<&'static str> = sides\n                    .iter()\n                    .map(|s| match s {\n                        Side::Code => \"code\",\n                        Side::Bot => \"bot\",\n                    })\n                    .collect();\n                f.write_str(&parts.join(\",\"))\n            }\n            Scope::Single(p) => write!(f, \"{}\", p.display()),\n        }\n    }\n}"
}
```

> DEVELOPER

Question, I'm surprized we need to do these "basic" operations, I would have thought these were already done when sync got --scope capabilities?

> AGENT

Good catch — fair question. The earlier sub-steps deliberately stayed minimal:

- **0.42.0-1** added the enum + helpers (no I/O).
- **0.42.0-2** added `parse_scope` (input direction only).
- **0.42.0-3** wired `sync` to `--scope`, but sync is one-shot — resolve scope at startup, run, exit. Nothing to serialize.

Push is the first consumer that **persists** scope, because the state machine resumes across invocations. If a user runs `vc-x1 push main --scope=code`, leaves, then comes back and runs bare `vc-x1 push`, the resume must know to stay code-only — otherwise it silently expands to the workspace default `code,bot` and clobbers the bot side. So push needs to round-trip `Scope` through `push-state.toml`, and that needs a stable string form (`Display` + `parse_scope` as inverses).

That's why this is hitting now and not in -3.

That said — there are other shapes if you'd rather:

1. **`Display` impl on `Scope`** (what I started). Symmetric with `parse_scope`; lets any future persister (cron-state, undo-anchor, etc.) reuse it.
2. **Push-private `scope_to_state_str` / `state_str_to_scope` helpers.** Keeps `scope.rs` purely about parsing/matching; serialization stays in push. Smaller blast radius if we ever want a different on-disk encoding for some future consumer.
3. **Persist the resolved paths (`Vec<String>`) instead of the expression.** Avoids the serialization layer entirely. But loses the intent — `code,bot` and `["./", "./.claude"]` resolve identically but mean different things at the matcher level (e.g., ochid-trailer logic looks at "are both sides in scope?", which is a `Scope` shape question, not a path-list question).

I leaned to (1) because it's the smallest surface change and matches `parse_scope`'s existing role. Does that read right to you, or want option (2) so this stays push-internal?

> DEVELOPER

hmm, does sync support --scope=<path>?

> AGENT

Yes — sync supports it via the `Single(_)` variant. From -3's smoke tests:

```
vc-x1 sync --check -s ./.claude → 1 repo (Single-mode resolves directly to that path)
vc-x1 sync --check -s code      → 1 repo (Roles via .vc-config.toml)
cd /tmp && vc-x1 sync --check    → POR detected, default Single(cwd)
```

But sync only exercises **parse → resolve → run**. It never serializes a `Scope` back out, so `Display` was never needed. The serialization gap is the part that's specifically push (and any other future stateful command).

So `parse_scope` + `scope_to_repos` are reused as-is in push — those *are* already done. The only "basic" addition is the inverse direction (`Scope → string`) for the state file. That's genuinely new because nothing before push needed to write a `Scope` back to disk.

Want me to keep `Display` on `Scope` (option 1) or hide the serialization in push as private helpers (option 2)?

> DEVELOPER

Sorry, what is the definition of serialize in this context

> AGENT

In this context, "serialize" just means **convert the in-memory `Scope` value to a string we can write to disk**, and "deserialize" means the inverse — read that string back and reconstruct the `Scope`.

Concrete example. After someone runs `vc-x1 push main --scope=code`, push's state file `.vc-x1/push-state.toml` looks like this today:

```toml
[push-state]
version = 1
stage = "commit-app"
bookmark = "main"
started_at = "2026-04-27T15:30:00Z"
app_chid = "abc123def456"
title = "feat: x"
body = "..."
```

To make resume scope-aware, I need to add one more line:

```toml
scope = "code"
```

That `"code"` string is the **serialized** form of the in-memory `Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Code])` value. When push resumes later, it reads the file, sees `scope = "code"`, and **deserializes** by calling `parse_scope("code")` to rebuild `Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Code])`.

The two directions:

- `Scope` → `String` (serialize): `format!("{}", scope)` if I add `Display`, or a private `scope_to_state_str(&scope)` helper.
- `String` → `Scope` (deserialize): `parse_scope(s)` — already exists.

So the question I asked earlier is just: where should the serialize half live?

- **Option 1:** `impl Display for Scope` in `scope.rs` — alongside `parse_scope`, paired symmetrically.
- **Option 2:** A private `scope_to_state_str` function inside `push.rs` — keeps `scope.rs` purely about parsing, and any other future persister would write its own.

Both produce the exact same on-disk string `"code"` / `"bot"` / `"code,bot"` / `"./mypath"`. The choice is just about where the function lives.

```
115	
116	/// CLI entry point for the `sync` subcommand.
117	///
118	/// Thin wrapper over `sync_repos` that resolves the `-R` flag into a
119	/// concrete repo list (falling back to the dual-repo default) and
120	/// forwards the rest of the args. Tests call `sync_repos` directly
121	/// with absolute fixture paths.
122	///
123	/// When `--quiet` is set, the global log filter is temporarily clamped
124	/// to `Warn` for the duration of the call and restored on return, so
125	/// `info!` calls throughout sync (plus any subprocess-stderr routed
126	/// through `common::run`) go dark. Errors still surface at `Warn` /
127	/// `Error` so script callers don't lose diagnostics.
128	pub fn sync(args: &SyncArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
129	    let repos = resolve_args_to_repos(args)?;
130	    if args.quiet {
131	        let prev = log::max_level();
132	        log::set_max_level(LevelFilter::Warn);
133	        let result = sync_repos(&repos, args);
134	        log::set_max_level(prev);
135	        result
136	    } else {
137	        sync_repos(&repos, args)
138	    }
139	}
140	
141	/// Sync the given repos against their remotes.
142	///
143	/// Orchestrates the full flow: pre-flight clean-check on every repo,
144	/// snapshot each repo's current op id, then hand off to `run_plan` for
145	/// fetch + classify + (optional) act. On any error, revert every repo
146	/// to its snapshot op via `jj op restore` so the caller sees an atomic
147	/// "either it all went through or nothing changed" outcome.
148	///
149	/// Paths may be relative (resolved against the process cwd) or
150	/// absolute. Tests use absolute tempdir paths to avoid cwd dependence
151	/// under parallel `cargo test`.
152	pub fn sync_repos(repos: &[PathBuf], args: &SyncArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
153	    debug!(
154	        "sync: enter (no_check={}, bookmark={}, remote={})",
155	        args.no_check, args.bookmark, args.remote
156	    );
157	
158	    // Preflight: verify bookmark tracking on every repo before any
159	    // fetch/rebase. Implements "Non-tracking-remote bookmark detection"
160	    // — design at:
161	    //   https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1/blob/main/notes/chores-06.md#non-tracking-remote-bookmark-detection-design
162	    for repo in repos {
163	        crate::common::verify_tracking(repo, &args.bookmark)?;
164	    }
165	
166	    let mut snapshots: Vec<(PathBuf, String)> = Vec::new();
167	    for repo in repos {
168	        let op_id = current_op_id(repo)?;
169	        debug!("{}: op snapshot = {op_id}", repo.display());
170	        snapshots.push((repo.clone(), op_id));
171	    }
172	
173	    let result = run_plan(&snapshots, args);
174	
175	    if let Err(e) = &result {
176	        warn!("sync failed: {e}");
177	        warn!("reverting all repos to starting state...");
178	        for (repo, op_id) in &snapshots {
179	            match op_restore(repo, op_id) {
180	                Ok(()) => info!("  {}: reverted to op {op_id}", repo.display()),
181	                Err(re) => warn!("  {}: revert failed: {re}", repo.display()),
182	            }
183	        }
184	    }
185	
186	    debug!("sync: exit");
187	    result
188	}
189	
190	/// Fetch and classify each repo, then act (or not, in dry-run).
191	///
192	/// Returns `Err` on the first failure so the caller can trigger the
193	/// cross-repo revert; partial success across repos is *not* a valid
194	/// end state for this command.
195	fn run_plan(
196	    snapshots: &[(PathBuf, String)],
197	    args: &SyncArgs,
198	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
199	    // Phase 1 — fetch + classify silently.
200	    //
201	    // Each repo's fetch stderr is captured (rather than streamed to
202	    // `info!` via `common::run`) so we can decide after classification
203	    // whether to surface it. `jj git fetch`'s routine "Nothing
204	    // changed." chatter is the main thing we're suppressing here —
205	    // if nothing needs action, the user shouldn't see it.
206	    let mut fetched: Vec<(PathBuf, String)> = Vec::new();
207	    let mut ctxs: Vec<RepoCtx> = Vec::new();
208	    for (repo, op_id) in snapshots {
209	        let stderr = fetch_silent(repo, &args.remote)?;
210	        fetched.push((repo.clone(), stderr));
211	        let state = classify(repo, &args.bookmark, &args.remote)?;
212	        ctxs.push(RepoCtx {
213	            path: repo.clone(),
214	            op_id: op_id.clone(),
215	            state,
216	        });
217	    }
218	
219	    let any_action_needed = ctxs
220	        .iter()
221	        .any(|c| matches!(c.state, State::Behind { .. } | State::Diverged { .. }));
222	
223	    // Phase 2 — emit status. `--quiet` is enforced globally via the
224	    // log-level clamp in `sync()`, so these `info!` calls are already
225	    // suppressed in scripts; we just shape the output here.
226	    if !any_action_needed {
227	        let n = ctxs.len();
228	        let noun = if n == 1 { "repo" } else { "repos" };
229	        info!("sync: {n} {noun}, all bookmarks up-to-date");
230	    } else {
231	        for (repo, stderr) in &fetched {
232	            info!("{}: fetch {}", repo.display(), args.remote);
233	            for line in stderr.lines() {
234	                info!("{line}");
235	            }
236	        }
237	        for ctx in &ctxs {
238	            log_state(&ctx.path, &ctx.state);
239	        }
240	    }
241	
242	    // Phase 3 — act (subprocess output streams through as usual).
243	    // `act_on_state` and `ensure_at_on_main` short-circuit when
244	    // `args.no_check` is false, so check mode is a true no-op here.
245	    for ctx in &ctxs {
246	        act_on_state(ctx, args)?;
247	        ensure_at_on_main(&ctx.path, &args.bookmark, args.no_check)?;
248	    }
249	
250	    // Phase 4 — check mode is fatal when action would be needed.
251	    // Apply mode (`--no-check`) ran the action above and is done.
252	    if !args.no_check && any_action_needed {
253	        let n_action = ctxs
254	            .iter()
255	            .filter(|c| matches!(c.state, State::Behind { .. } | State::Diverged { .. }))
256	            .count();
257	        let noun = if n_action == 1 { "repo" } else { "repos" };
258	        return Err(format!(
259	            "sync: {n_action} {noun} need action (see above) — \
260	             resolve with `vc-x1 sync --no-check` and re-run"
261	        )
262	        .into());
263	    }
264	    Ok(())
265	}
266	
267	/// Fetch `repo` from `remote` without streaming subprocess output to
268	/// `info!`.
269	///
270	/// Mirrors what `common::run` would do, but returns stderr to the
271	/// caller so the caller can decide whether to surface it (verbose /
272	/// action case) or drop it (clean case). Stdout is dropped — `jj git
273	/// fetch` doesn't use it. Failure carries stderr in the error message.
274	fn fetch_silent(repo: &Path, remote: &str) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
275	    debug!("$ jj git fetch --remote {remote} -R {}", repo.display());
276	    let output = Command::new("jj")
277	        .args(["git", "fetch", "--remote", remote, "-R", &repo_str(repo)])
278	        .output()
279	        .map_err(|e| format!("failed to run jj git fetch: {e}"))?;
280	    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout).trim().to_string();
281	    let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr).trim().to_string();
282	    if !stdout.is_empty() {
283	        debug!("  {stdout}");
284	    }
285	    if !output.status.success() {
286	        return Err(format!("jj git fetch -R {} failed: {stderr}", repo.display()).into());
287	    }
288	    Ok(stderr)
289	}
290	
291	/// Ensure `@` is a descendant of `bookmark`, rebasing if not.
292	///
293	/// `jj git fetch` fast-forwards a tracked local bookmark to the remote
294	/// tip when local is a strict ancestor, but leaves `@` behind — still
295	/// parented on the *pre-fetch* bookmark commit. The `.claude` repo is
296	/// the motivating case: trailing session writes (e.g. `/exit`'s jsonl
297	/// tail) sit in `@`, and without this step they'd end up orphaned on a
298	/// stale branch when the remote advanced.
299	///
300	/// Skipped when `main::@` is already non-empty (i.e., `@` is already
301	/// reachable from `bookmark`). On `--no-check`, runs
302	/// `jj rebase -b @ -d <bookmark>` which carries all commits between
303	/// the old parent and `@` forward, then checks `conflicts()` — any
304	/// conflict in this step trips the outer revert the same way a
305	/// conflicted `act_on_state` rebase would.
306	fn ensure_at_on_main(
307	    repo: &Path,
308	    bookmark: &str,
309	    apply: bool,
310	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
311	    if revset_nonempty(repo, &format!("{bookmark}::@"))? {
312	        return Ok(());
313	    }
314	    info!("{}: rebasing @ onto '{bookmark}'", repo.display());
```

Good catch on the gap. Let me check honestly: the `--check` vs `--no-check` distinction in sync is just whether `act_on_state` mutates or short-circuits — both modes go through the same `resolve_args_to_repos → sync_repos → run_plan` chain, so the scope plumbing is the same regardless. There's no code path that would treat `--scope=<path>` differently between modes.

But you're right that **empirically** -3 only smoke-tested `--check`. We haven't actually run `vc-x1 sync --no-check -s ./somepath` to confirm it works end-to-end. The reasoning is "the code paths are uniform so it should," not "we proved it."

Want me to:

1. **Pause -4 and validate sync's `--no-check -s <path>` first** (say, against a throwaway fixture or the live `.claude` repo if you trust it as a single target). If anything breaks, fix it as a quick -3 follow-up before -4.
2. **Continue -4 and trust the code structure.** Push will exercise `--scope=<path>` more thoroughly via its dogfood validation in -7, which will surface anything sync missed.

I lean toward (1) — five minutes of validation now beats discovering a sync bug from inside push later. Your call.

```
332	/// Perform the mutation corresponding to `ctx.state` when `--no-check`.
333	///
334	/// - `UpToDate` / `Ahead` / `NoRemote` → no-op (and no output, the state
335	///   was already logged by `log_state`).
336	/// - `Behind` → `jj bookmark set <b> -r <b>@<remote>` to fast-forward.
337	/// - `Diverged` → `jj rebase -b <b> -d <b>@<remote>`, then probe
338	///   `conflicts()`. A non-empty result means the rebase produced
339	///   conflicted commits; return `Err` so the outer revert restores the
340	///   pre-fetch state.
341	fn act_on_state(ctx: &RepoCtx, args: &SyncArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
```

> DEVELOPER

If you use `sync --check` there should no changes, I would hope it would use something none distructive. ut maybe it's a chicken and egg problem.

> AGENT

Right — `sync --check` should be observation-only. The destructive bit is `jj git fetch`, which auto-fast-forwards any tracked local bookmark whose remote has advanced. That's a jj behavior, not a vc-x1 choice. Sync's classify then sees the post-fetch state and reports "up-to-date" when really it should have reported "behind" pre-fetch.

The chicken-and-egg framing is real, but there are ways out:

1. **Pre-check via `git ls-remote`** — cheapest. Compare local `main`'s commit-id to `origin`'s without touching jj state:
   - tips equal → "up-to-date", no fetch needed.
   - tips differ → fetch to classify (now we *know* a state change is happening; --check can refuse before fetching, or fetch and report).

   This makes the pure-up-to-date case truly non-destructive.

2. **Fetch into a holding ref**: `git fetch origin main:refs/vc-x1-tmp/main` lands the remote tip in a vc-x1-internal ref without touching jj's tracked bookmark. Classify + report, then either advance (`--no-check`) or delete the holding ref (`--check`). More plumbing, more bullet-proof.

3. **Op-restore wrap**: `jj op snapshot → fetch → classify → if --check, op restore`. Simplest in code but moves jj's op log around for every check — feels noisy.

Whichever, it's a pre-existing sync issue (would surface identically with `--scope=code` in any single-repo workspace; not introduced by `-3` and not specific to `--scope=<path>`). It doesn't block push --scope — push's preflight runs `vc-x1 sync --check`, gets the same silent FF, and the post-fetch state is still consistent for the subsequent commit/push stages.

Three paths I see:

- **A.** Note the bug as a follow-up todo, proceed with -4 push --scope. Sync gets a separate cycle (-5 or 0.43.0) to fix the destructiveness properly.
- **B.** Pause -4, slot in a "sync --check non-destructive" sub-step before push's wiring (since push's preflight depends on sync's correctness).
- **C.** Bundle it into -4 (sync fix + push --scope in one commit).

I lean **A** — the bug is annoying but not corruption-class; push works fine on top of today's sync; mixing a sync-internals fix into push-scope work conflates two concerns. Your call.

Test repos at `/tmp/vc-x1-sync-validate` left intact pending your direction.

> AGENT

Agreed it's a fundamental sync issue — masquerading as observation while silently advancing tracked bookmarks. Here's my proposed plan:

---

## Plan: 0.41.1 — sync `--check` non-destructive

### Bug summary

`vc-x1 sync --check` is documented as observation-only. In practice when local main is behind origin:

1. `jj git fetch` runs and **auto-fast-forwards** the local tracked bookmark (jj behavior, not vc-x1's).
2. `classify` reads the post-fetch state, sees `local == remote`, reports "all bookmarks up-to-date".
3. `ensure_at_on_main` sees `main::@` empty (because `@` is now orphaned on the pre-fetch branch), prints "rebasing @ onto 'main'", but skips the actual rebase under `apply=false`.
4. Net: bookmark silently moved, `@` orphaned, log lies.

Reproduced 2026-04-27 against a POR fixture (`/tmp/vc-x1-sync-validate`, still on disk). Pre-dates 0.42.0 — any tracked-bookmark sync is affected; Single mode just makes the smallest reproduction.

### Branch + version policy

- **Branch off the 0.41.0 release commit** (`6747a27`). Single-step cycle (focused fix).
- Bump to `0.41.1`.
- After 0.41.1 ships, forward-merge into main; the merge lands as a 0.42.0-X follow-up step or folds into -4's pre-work, depending on conflict size with the Single-mode code in -3.
- (Open question on branch naming — suggest `0.41.1-sync-check`.)

### Fix design — two options

**Option A: `git ls-remote` pre-check, no fetch in --check mode.**
- New helper `remote_tip(repo, remote, bookmark)` shelling `git ls-remote --heads`.
- `--check` branch: compare local tip vs remote tip. Equal → "up-to-date". Differ → "needs sync, run `--no-check`" and exit non-zero. **No fetch, no `ensure_at_on_main` call.**
- `--no-check` branch: today's flow unchanged.
- *Pros:* tiny diff; `--check` becomes truly observation-only; preserves classify code untouched.
- *Cons:* `--check` no longer reports behind-vs-diverged distinction. (My read: that's fine — `--check` is a gate, `--no-check` is the diagnostic-and-action.)

**Option B: Holding-ref fetch.**
- `git fetch <remote> <bk>:refs/vc-x1/holding/<bk>` lands the remote tip in a private ref namespace, leaving jj's bookmark untouched.
- Classify against the holding ref. `--check` deletes the ref after; `--no-check` explicitly moves the bookmark + cleans up.
- *Pros:* full classification detail in `--check`.
- *Cons:* more plumbing, ref-namespace cleanup hygiene, two bookmark-advance paths.

**Recommend Option A.** Smallest change for the largest correctness win; can graduate to Option B later if `--check` gains diagnostic responsibilities.

### Edits (Option A)

- `Cargo.toml`: 0.41.0 → 0.41.1.
- `src/sync.rs`: add `remote_tip` helper; split `sync_repos` (or `run_plan`) into a check-path and a no-check-path; remove the `apply`-flag branch inside `ensure_at_on_main` (now only ever called under `--no-check`).
- New integration test mirroring today's POR validation: assert `--check` leaves the repo unmutated when behind and errors with the "run --no-check" hint.
- `notes/chores-06.md` (or a new `notes/chores-06b.md`): "0.41.1: sync --check non-destructive" subsection.
- `notes/todo.md`: Done entry under the 0.41.0 line.

### Behavior consequence to flag

`vc-x1 push`'s preflight calls `vc-x1 sync --check`. After the fix, preflight will **refuse** to proceed when local is behind, instead of silently advancing the bookmark via fetch and continuing. That's the *correct* new behavior — push should never silently move the user's bookmark — but it's a deliberate semantics change worth calling out.

### Open questions

1. **Branch name.** `0.41.1-sync-check`? Or shorter (`fix/sync-check`)?
2. **`--check` on network failure** (ls-remote non-zero exit). Lean: hard error. "I can't tell" ≠ "up-to-date".
3. **Forward-merge timing.** Apply the fix to main as a discrete 0.42.0-3.1 step, or fold into -4? Simpler bookkeeping if discrete.
4. **0.42.0-4 (push --scope)** — pause entirely until 0.41.1 lands, or keep the WIP (Cargo.toml bump + scope.rs Display tweak) on a separate branch and resume after merge?

Thoughts before I touch anything?

The proposed sequence is:

1. `init --scope=bot ../tmp-vc-x1` → expect error, no dir
2. `init --scope=code ../tmp-vc-x1` → expect single-repo, no `.claude/`
3. `init --scope=bot ../tmp-vc-x1` → expect "full dual-repo"

**Step 3 reads as `bot` again, not `code,bot`.** Two interpretations:

- **(a) Typo**, meant `--scope=code,bot` from a clean dir → would just be a re-run of Test #2 (already verified).
- **(b) Aspirational**, you want `init --scope=bot` on top of an existing code-only workspace to mean "**add the bot side** and complete the dual-repo."

I'd bet (b), based on your earlier "I'm inclined to have init support all modes of scope." The proposed test would then **surface a gap**: today the binary will fail step 3 — either with `--scope=bot` fatal at plan time ("bot-only meaningless without code"), or with `repoA already exists` from init's pre-flight, depending on which check fires first. We'd document the gap in the notes file as a missing feature.

Either reading is useful to run; the meaning of "verify full dual-repo" differs:

- If (a): "verify dual-repo" = same checks as Test #2.
- If (b): "verify dual-repo" = expected outcome that today's binary won't deliver — the test demonstrates the gap.

Which interpretation? Or both — interpret literally as (b), expect failure, document, then re-run as (a) on a clean dir to confirm the "fresh dual" path still works?

> AGENT

Notes updated with Test 8 + a "Findings: path-form support is wanted" section that lays out two candidate semantics and recommends (a) — `init NAME --scope=<path>` ≡ `init NAME --repo-local=<path> --scope=code`.

Now the design discussion. Two related questions before drafting an implementation plan:

**1. Single-repo only, or also dual-repo?**

`--scope=<path>` in the `Single(_)` semantic strongly implies single-repo (it's the "ignore the dual defaults" escape hatch). But there's a nearby interpretation: `--scope=<path>` chooses the **location**, the existing `--scope=code` / `--scope=code,bot` keyword chooses the **mode**, and the two could combine:

- `--scope=<path>` (alone) → mode defaults to single-repo (`code`), at `<path>/NAME`.
- `--scope=<path> --some-other-flag-meaning-also-bot`? → mode upgraded to dual at `<path>/NAME`?

Lean: **don't** allow combining for now. `--scope` is one slot; one value; one semantic. Path form ⇒ single-repo. Keyword form ⇒ dual or single based on keyword. Keeps the surface narrow. If the user later wants `init --repo-local=<path> --scope=code,bot` to combine "location" + "mode", that already exists (and is the current way).

**2. Conflict matrix with existing flags.**

`--scope=<path>` would conflict with:
- `--repo-local` (both pick a local location).
- `--repo-remote` (path form is local-only).
- `--owner` / `--private` (GitHub-only flags).
- `--dir` (parent dir; `<path>` *is* the parent).

All of these would need to become "fatal with `--scope=<path>`" — same shape as today's `--repo-local` exclusions. Mechanical but real.

**3. Roadmap implication.**

Reordering: path-form `init` first, then 0.41.1 sync fix. That bumps the version cycle structure to:

- Some `0.41.1` (or `0.42.0-3.x`?) cycle for path-form init — it's a feature, not a fix.
- Then `0.41.x` (or `0.41.2`?) for the sync `--check` fix.
- Then resume `0.42.0-4` push --scope on top of both.

Question: does path-form-init belong as a 0.41.x patch (since it's an extension of 0.41.0's `--scope` work), or as part of the in-flight 0.42.0 enum-refactor cycle (since `Single(_)` is a 0.42.0 type-model construct)? My lean: **0.42.0-3.1** as a step inside the in-flight cycle — it directly extends the `Single(_)` enum work that just landed in `-2`/`-3`.

Want me to draft the formal plan, or are there design points to tighten first?

> DEVELOPER

Let's start with first principles, what do we want to support?

> AGENT

Right, let me lay out the substrate so we're choosing on the same map.

## What shapes of repo/workspace exist?

| Shape | `.vc-config.toml` |
|---|---|
| **POR** | absent |
| **Single-repo workspace** | `path = "/"`, no `other-repo` |
| **Dual-repo workspace (code side)** | `path = "/"`, `other-repo = ".claude"` |
| **Dual-repo workspace (bot side)** | `path = "/.claude"`, `other-repo = ".."` |

## What state transitions might init need?

| From | To | Operation |
|---|---|---|
| nothing | single-repo | **create** |
| nothing | dual-repo | **create** |
| single-repo | dual-repo | **upgrade** (add bot side, rewrite parent config, ochid-link) |
| POR | single-repo | **adopt** (write `.vc-config.toml`) |
| POR | dual-repo | **adopt + upgrade** |
| dual-repo | single-repo | **downgrade** (rare; demolition risk — probably out of scope) |
| any | (idempotent verify) | "I think it's set up already; tell me if it's not what I asked for" |

Today's binary supports **only** the top two — both create-from-scratch. Everything else fails.

## Two orthogonal axes init has to specify

- **Location** — where the workspace lives.
  - GitHub default (`--owner`, `--private`)
  - Existing remote (`--repo-remote=URL`)
  - Local fixture (`--repo-local=PATH`)
- **Mode (desired final shape)** — single-repo vs dual-repo.
  - Today: `--scope=code | code,bot` (and `bot` errors).

These are independent today, and they should probably stay that way — they answer different questions.

## So what's `--scope=<path>` for, in init specifically?

In sync / push / finalize, `Single(<path>)` is the *escape hatch from `.vc-config.toml` lookup* — "I don't care what the workspace says, just operate on this one repo." That semantic doesn't translate to init, because init is **writing** the config, not consulting it. There's nothing to escape from.

So path-form in init has two coherent readings, both of which are **shorthand**, not new semantic:

- **(a) Location shorthand.** `--scope=<path>` ≡ `--repo-local=<path>` + (mode defaults to single-repo). Folds location + mode into the one slot. Path is the parent; `NAME` positional still gives the project dir.
- **(b) Workspace-path shorthand.** `--scope=<path>` ≡ "the workspace literally lives at `<path>`." Replaces `NAME` *and* `--repo-local`. Conflicts with `NAME` positional today.

## Decision points

Before I draft a plan I'd want to lock these:

**Q1.** Do we want init to grow upgrade semantics at all (single→dual, POR→single, POR→dual)? Or stay create-only and let path-form be a pure typing-shorthand for the create-only case?

**Q2.** If we do upgrade semantics, what's the trigger? Auto-detect target state and pick op? Or explicit flag (`--upgrade`)? Auto-detect is friendlier but means `init` becomes harder to predict from the command line alone.

**Q3.** For path-form scope: reading (a) (location shorthand) or (b) (workspace-path replacement)? My lean is (a) — keeps `NAME` positional, drops to single-repo by default, doesn't conflict with the existing flag set.

**Q4.** What does `--scope=bot` mean if upgrade semantics exist?
- Today: fatal.
- With upgrade: "ensure bot side; require code side already present" — natural fit for the single→dual upgrade.

**Q5.** Idempotency. Should `init --scope=code` against an already-single-repo workspace no-op silently, or error "already exists"? (Consistency with `mkdir -p` argues for no-op; today errors at preflight.)

Pick directions on these and I can draft the plan.

> AGENT

Like the shape — scope = mode (5 values), location = required two-way choice. It naturally drops the `--scope=<path>` overload from init (path-form stays in sync/push/finalize where `Single(_)` is the "ignore-config" escape hatch — meaningless when init is *writing* the config).

Five points worth nailing down before this is a complete design:

**1. POR semantic — confirm.**
`--scope=por` produces: jj+git initialized at `<repo-local>/NAME` (or cloned from `<repo-remote>`) + bare remote on the local-fixture path + bookmark `main` set & tracked + **no** `.vc-config.toml`, no `.claude/`, no symlink, no cross-ref. Essentially today's `--scope=code` minus the `.vc-config.toml` write. Right?

**2. Which command does the `code → code,bot` upgrade?**

Two coherent assignments:

- **(i) `--scope=bot` is the upgrade trigger.** Read literally: "ensure bot side exists." Errors if no existing code-side workspace. `--scope=code,bot` stays strict-create (today's "already exists" error preserved).
- **(ii) `--scope=code,bot` is idempotent.** Detects existing state: empty → create; single-repo → upgrade; dual-repo → no-op. `--scope=bot` either becomes a synonym for the upgrade case or stays fatal-when-incoherent.

(ii) is friendlier (one flag covers all three create/upgrade/no-op cases for the dual-repo target) but less predictable from the command line. (i) is more explicit but means the user has to know which subcommand-shaped flag to use.

My lean: **(i)**. Mirrors what the user already wrote in the table — "code → code,bot via `--scope=bot`" reads naturally as "add the bot side." Idempotency for `--scope=code,bot` on existing-dual would still be desirable (point 5 below).

**3. POR upgrades.**
By symmetry with #2:
- `POR → code` via `--scope=code` (write `.vc-config.toml`, no other changes).
- `POR → code,bot` via `--scope=code,bot` (write parent config, add `.claude/`, ochid-link).
- `POR → bot` is incoherent (bot needs code-side first; on POR there is no code-side identified).

**4. The "location required" rule eliminates today's GitHub default mode.**
Today `vc-x1 init repoA --owner=me` creates a GitHub repo without `--repo-local` or `--repo-remote`. The proposed rule breaks that. Is that intentional?

Three options:
- (a) Keep GitHub-default — `--owner` implies "create new GitHub repo," location is implicitly the cwd or `--dir`. So location is "one of `--repo-remote`, `--repo-local`, or implicit GitHub via `--owner`."
- (b) Drop GitHub-default — require `--repo-remote` even for new GitHub repos (user creates the repo on GitHub first, then `init`'s against the URL).
- (c) Keep GitHub-default but add a third explicit form: `--repo-github=<owner>/<name>` to make "create on GitHub" symmetric with the others.

My lean: (a). Don't break working invocations.

**5. Idempotency policy.**
`init --scope=X` against a workspace already in state X — no-op silently, log a "already in target state", or error?

My lean: **no-op + log** ("already in desired state at `<path>`"). `mkdir -p` semantics — friendlier than today's hard error. But this is a user-experience call.

Subject to those five, the design holds together cleanly. Pick directions and I'll draft the formal plan (still no code).

Three possible reads:

- **(A)** That's fine — single-repo workspace was a low-priority shape; drop #2 from "must work." Rationale: POR + (later) a one-shot `.vc-config.toml` write covers any real need; init shouldn't carry a flag for the rare case.
- **(B)** Keep #2 reachable via a different command — e.g. a future `vc-x1 adopt` that promotes a POR to a single-repo workspace by writing `.vc-config.toml { path = "/" }`. Init stays simple; adoption is a separate primitive.
- **(C)** Compromise — keep `code` in the init menu after all, accept three values: `code,bot | code | por`. Costs the simplification you're proposing.

If you go (A) or (B), the simplified menu also implies init becomes **idempotent + state-aware** for the upgrade cases:

- `--scope=code,bot` on empty → create dual.
- `--scope=code,bot` on POR → upgrade (write configs, add `.claude/`, ochid-link).
- `--scope=code,bot` on existing dual → no-op.
- `--scope=por` on empty → create POR.
- `--scope=por` on POR → no-op.
- `--scope=por` on existing workspace → **error** (downgrade not handled here).

Question: which read — (A), (B), or (C)?

Locked. Summary of init's design:

**`--scope` menu** (others error):

- empty → POR (`--scope=por`)
- empty → dual (`--scope=code,bot`)

**One last design question I haven't seen you address:** the GitHub default mode.

Today, `vc-x1 init repoA --owner=me` works *without* `--repo-local` or `--repo-remote` — it creates a fresh GitHub repo on the fly. Your earlier "location required" rule said one of `--repo-remote`, `--repo-local` must be passed, which would drop that mode.

Three options:

- **(a)** Keep GitHub-default: location is "one of `--repo-remote`, `--repo-local`, **or** implicit-via-`--owner`." No invocation breaks.
- **(b)** Drop GitHub-default: require `--repo-remote=<URL>` even for new GitHub repos (user pre-creates on GitHub, then `init`s against the URL). Smaller flag set; one fewer code path; but breaks `--owner` workflow.
- **(c)** Add explicit `--repo-github=<owner>/<name>` as a third location form, drop the implicit-via-`--owner` magic. Symmetric with the other two.

Lean **(a)** — fewest invocation breaks, smallest scope. (c) has design appeal but expands surface area.

Pick, then I draft the plan.

On "jj not installed", I think we should verify and error nicely with links to jj install instructions
do we support `vc-x1 init git@github.com:winksaville/xyz-repo` so no owner or repo-remote?

> AGENT

**On jj-not-installed:** Agree — preflight should `which jj` (or `jj --version`) and error with a friendly message linking to https://martinvonz.github.io/jj/latest/install-and-setup/ if missing. Fail fast at preflight before any operation, not at the moment we shell out to `jj git init`.

**On positional URL form:** Today doesn't support it. The current model:

- Positional `[NAME]` is treated as a project name only — passing a URL there would attempt to use `git@github.com:winksaville/xyz-repo` as a project name (and probably error somewhere downstream).
- The way to clone from a URL today is `vc-x1 init --repo-remote=git@github.com:winksaville/xyz-repo.git` (NAME omitted; init derives it from the URL's last segment).

The shorthand `vc-x1 init git@github.com:...` would require **autodetection** on the positional: URL-shaped input → set `--repo-remote` implicitly, NAME derived from URL; bare-name input → today's NAME behavior.

Detection is tractable — URL forms are distinct (`://`, or SSH `<host>:<path>` shape with `@`). False positives unlikely (a project legitimately named `foo:bar/baz` is a corner case the user can resolve by passing `--repo-remote` explicitly).

**Worth it?** The shorthand removes one flag for the most-common GitHub clone case and reads naturally (`vc-x1 init <repo-url>` parallels `git clone <repo-url>`). It does add:
- ~10 lines of URL-shape detection.
- A subtle ambiguity ("what is positional?" depends on its content).
- One more variant in the location-precedence rules: positional URL vs `--repo-remote` flag — same source of truth, but two ways to express it.

Three paths:

- **(a)** Add URL autodetection to this init plan. Small extra scope, real UX win, fits the "simplify" theme (fewer flags to type for the common case).
- **(b)** Defer. Land the scope simplification first; URL shorthand as a separate small follow-up cycle.
- **(c)** Skip. `--repo-remote=<URL>` is good enough; one less detection corner.

Lean **(a)** — small enough to fit, clear UX win, and the GitHub-default question (option (a)/(b)/(c) from my last message) interacts: if we add positional URL support, then we don't need `--repo-remote` for the common case, and the GitHub-default question gets simpler too.

Pick on URL shorthand + the GitHub-default question (a/b/c), then I draft.

> AGENT

Not mad — this is the most coherent simplification yet. Single positional + one flag. The mental model collapses to:

```
vc-x1 init <URL-or-path> [--scope=code,bot|por]
```

- URL form → clone existing remote into cwd-derived subdir, set up workspace on top.
- Path form → create at that path locally.
- `--scope` chooses shape (defaults to `code,bot`).

Test #2's `vc-x1 init repoA --repo-local=../tmp-vc-x1` becomes `vc-x1 init ../tmp-vc-x1/repoA`. Test #1's becomes `vc-x1 init ../tmp-vc-x1/repoA --scope=por`. Much cleaner.

Two real costs / questions to surface:

**Cost 1: Lose GitHub-create-on-the-fly.**
Today `vc-x1 init repoA --owner=me` creates a fresh GitHub repo + local workspace + initial push, all in one command. In the URL-or-path model, the GitHub repo must exist first. The workflow becomes two steps:
1. Create empty repo on GitHub (manually via `gh` CLI or web).
2. `vc-x1 init git@github.com:me/repoA`.

Acceptable trade for the simplification, in my read — but flagging because it's a real workflow regression for anyone bootstrapping new projects regularly.

**Question 1: where do bare remotes go in the path form?**

Today's `--repo-local=<parent>` model puts the working repo at `<parent>/<NAME>` and the bare remote at `<parent>/remote.git` (or `remote-code.git` + `remote-claude.git` for dual). The parent dir is explicitly a "fixture root" the user dedicates to the test setup.

In the new `vc-x1 init <path>` model, the positional IS the workspace path. Where do bare remotes land?

- **(i)** Adjacent — `<path>/../remote-*.git`. Same as today, but the parent dir is implicit. **Surprising** if the user picks a busy parent dir.
- **(ii)** Nested — `<path>/.bare/code.git` and `<path>/.bare/claude.git` inside the workspace. Self-contained, no parent-dir surprises. Slightly weird (bare remotes inside the working tree), but `.gitignore`'d cleanly.
- **(iii)** None by default — path-form init creates the working repo only, no bare remote. To get a bare remote (for fixtures / push testing), opt in via `--with-bare-remote=<path>` or similar.

**Lean (ii)** — self-contained workspaces are the cleanest mental model; "everything for this workspace lives under `<path>/`." Tests/fixtures can still reach the bare via `<path>/.bare/code.git`. No parent-dir pollution.

**(iii)** is also defensible if you want path-form to be minimal and remote-setup to be explicit. The cost is `Fixture` in `src/test_helpers.rs` would need to pass an extra flag.

Pick on URL-or-path positional (yes/no) + bare-remote location, then I draft.

> AGENT

Right — I overcorrected. `winksaville/NAME` is a perfectly serviceable shorthand for "create on GitHub" and preserves the workflow. Three positional forms then:

| Positional shape | Meaning | Bare remote |
|---|---|---|
| `git@github.com:owner/name` or `https://...` (has `://` or SSH `<host>:<path>` with `@`) | clone existing remote | the URL |
| `owner/name` (exactly one `/`, no path prefix) | create on GitHub, clone, set up | created on GitHub |
| `./X` / `../X` / `/X` / `~/X` / `~` (path-prefixed) | local fixture | needs an answer (see below) |

**Bare name (no slash, no path prefix)** like `vc-x1 init repoA` — error with hint: "ambiguous: prefix with `./repoA` for local, `owner/repoA` for GitHub, or pass full URL." Mirrors `parse_scope`'s "no bare names" rule for `--scope=<path>`.

**Detection precedence:**
1. URL-shape → URL form.
2. Single `/`, no path prefix → owner/name (GitHub-create).
3. Path-prefix → local path.
4. Otherwise → error.

**Flags that survive:**
- `--scope=code,bot|por` (default `code,bot`) — shape.
- `--private` — only meaningful with owner/name form (public vs private GitHub repo).
- `--dry-run` — preview without acting.

**Flags that die:**
- `--owner` — encoded in positional.
- `--repo-local` / `--repo-remote` — encoded in positional.
- `--dir` — superseded by the positional path form.
- `NAME` positional in the old sense — replaced by the unified positional.

**The one remaining design call:** bare remote location for the path form.

- **(i)** Adjacent — `<parent-of-path>/remote-*.git`. Same as today; surprising if `<parent>` is busy.
- **(ii)** Nested — `<path>/.bare/code.git` (+ `claude.git` for dual). Workspace is self-contained.
- **(iii)** None — path form just creates the working repo. Add a separate flag (e.g. `--with-bare`) to opt into bare-remote creation. Most minimal.

Lean (ii) — self-contained workspaces are the cleanest mental model; everything related to `<path>` lives under `<path>/`. Tests reach the bare at `<path>/.bare/code.git` (one extra path component vs today, but explicit).

Pick on the bare-remote location, then I draft.

```
                (errors if the host isn't one we know how to create on)
```

Both URL form and `owner/name` form go through the same path. `owner/name` is just `git@github.com:owner/name.git` with the prefix elided.

That preserves the one-shot GitHub-create workflow I'd written off — `vc-x1 init winksaville/foo` either clones if `foo` exists or creates+clones if not. `--private` survives as a meta flag for the create case (if remote already exists, `--private` is silently irrelevant — minor footgun worth a warn).

**Two new minor concerns from this unification:**

1. **Typo footgun.** `vc-x1 init winksaville/fooo` (typo) silently creates a *new* `fooo` repo. Mitigated by `--dry-run` showing "will create new repo: foo**o**" before doing it. But the unprompted `vc-x1 init winksaville/fooo` would still go through. Lean: accept the tradeoff, document it. The user owns their typos.

2. **Non-GitHub remotes.** `git@gitlab.com:x/y` that doesn't exist → can't auto-create (no gitlab integration). Error must be friendly: "remote does not exist and auto-create not supported for this host; create the remote manually first."

---

So the summary now:

```
vc-x1 init <positional> [--scope=code,bot|por] [--private] [--dry-run]
```

Three positional shapes (URL / `owner/name` / path-prefixed). Bare-name positional errors with a hint. Remote forms: clone-or-create. Path form: local fixture.

**Still need a call on bare-remote location for path form** — (i) adjacent in parent dir, (ii) nested under `<path>/.bare/`, (iii) opt-in via separate flag. Lean (ii). Pick that and I'll draft.

With (ii) .bare should/must be in .gitignore?

> AGENT

Yes, must — otherwise `git status` from `<path>/` shows the entire bare repo as untracked, and `git add .` would happily stage it. Same for `jj`'s view.

Init's existing `.gitignore`-write step picks up one more line:

```
# code-side <path>/.gitignore
/.claude/      # already there today (for dual)
/.vc-x1/       # already there today (push state)
/.bare/        # new — local fixture bares
```

The bot side's `<path>/.claude/.gitignore` doesn't need a `.bare/` entry because `.bare/` lives at the workspace root (outside the bot repo's working tree) — bot's view is `../.bare/`, beyond its scope.

For `--scope=por` with bare: same `<path>/.gitignore` gets `/.bare/`.

**Small additional argument this raises for the option-tree:**

- **(i) adjacent** — no gitignore concern at all (bares live in a sibling dir, not in any working tree).
- **(ii) nested** — needs the new `.gitignore` line; small mechanical work but real.
- **(iii) opt-in flag** — no gitignore concern when not used; needed when used (same as ii).

The gitignore work for (ii) is trivial (one line in the gitignore-write helper), so it doesn't shift my lean — still **(ii)** for self-containment. Just confirming you see the small cost.
